## PROJECT IDENTITY

PROJECT NAME:
CampusHub

CampusHub: A Campus Marketplace & Facilities Booking System with Real-Time Availability Monitoring and Analytics Dashboard

PROJECT:
BSIT Capstone
USTP Oroquieta City
2026

CampusHub combines:

## CORE ARCHITECTURE

PRIMARY BACKEND:
Django + Django REST Framework

PRIMARY DATABASE:
PostgreSQL

MOBILE:
Flutter

FLUTTER STATE MANAGEMENT:
GetX where appropriate

REAL-TIME:
FastAPI mainly for real-time chat/messaging.

DATABASE ADMINISTRATION:
pgAdmin

SOURCE CONTROL:
Git + GitHub

AI / ANALYTICS DEVELOPMENT:
Python
pandas
mlxtend
openpyxl
Google Colab

IMPORTANT ARCHITECTURE RULE:

Django + Django REST Framework + PostgreSQL are the main source of truth.

Do NOT migrate core functionality to FastAPI without a strong architectural reason.

Keep these primarily in Django:

- Payments
- Sellers
- Users
- Authentication
- RBAC
- Facilities

- Reports

FastAPI is mainly intended for real-time messaging/chat.

## DESIGN SYSTEM

CampusHub branding:

Primary Navy:
#1A1851

#FCB316

White:
#FFFFFF

Montserrat

DESIGN DIRECTION:

- Clean

- Consistent

- Accessible

Avoid:

- excessive gradients

- huge border radius

Use semantic colors carefully.

restrained positive color

CampusHub gold / attention color

Rejected / Cancelled / Out of Stock:
restrained red

Archived / Inactive:
muted gray/navy

Do not communicate status using color alone.

Always include:
text + icon/indicator where applicable.

## GLOBAL DASHBOARD DATE FILTER

Recommended options:

- Today
- Last 7 Days
- Last 30 Days
- This Month
- This Year
- Custom Range

Default:
Last 30 Days

There are TWO kinds of KPI.

CURRENT SNAPSHOT KPIs:

- Total Users
- Active Sellers
- Total Products
- Total Facilities
- Pending Approvals

Their primary value should represent the current state.

Their secondary text may reference the selected period.

Examples:

TOTAL USERS
125
+8 new in selected period

TOTAL PRODUCTS
246
+15 added in selected period


PERIOD-BASED KPIs:

- Total Orders
- Total Bookings
- Marketplace Revenue

These should recalculate based on the selected date range.

Examples:

TOTAL ORDERS
42
↑ 16.7% vs previous 30 days

MARKETPLACE REVENUE
₱32,450.00
↑ 8.4% vs previous 30 days

## KPI TREND RULES

Percentage change:

((current - previous) / previous) * 100

Handle previous = 0 safely.

Do NOT show:

Infinity%
NaN%

If previous = 0 and current > 0:
show:
"New activity"
or equivalent.

If both = 0:
show:
"No change"
or a more useful zero-state description.

Do not make the whole KPI card green or red.

Main KPI value stays navy.

Trend indicator can be:
- green positive
- red negative
- gold attention
- gray/navy neutral

Remember:

Revenue ↑
can be positive.

Cancelled Orders ↑
is negative.

Pending Approvals ↑
is attention, not positive green.

Low Stock ↑
is attention/negative.

Priority:

1. Main value
2. KPI label
3. Secondary/trend text
4. Icon

KPI label:
11–12px
semibold
navy

24–30px
bold
navy

11–12px
muted navy/gray

Keep all cards:

- equal height
- equal padding

- consistent label position
- consistent number baseline
- consistent secondary text alignment

Recommended position:

System Overview
↓

Do not fake unsupported alerts.

Each item should link to the correct module/filter.

System Status should monitor:

2. Chat Service

DATABASE
● Online
Database connection verified.

CHAT SERVICE

Real-time messaging available.

BACKUP

Last backup: ...
Open Backup →

Prefer a specific action such as:

Sep 05–11
sales = 0
orders = 0

sales = 0
orders = 0

show an error state.

show zero-value graph.

## TOP 5 BEST-SELLING PRODUCTS

This feature is NOT Apriori.

It does NOT require product pairs.

Top-Selling Products ranks INDIVIDUAL products based on actual valid completed sales.

A product sold alone still counts.

Example:

Order 001:
Burger x2

Order 002:
Burger x1

Order 003:
Coke x1

Result:

Burger = 3 sold
Coke = 1 sold

Ranking:

Primary:
Total Quantity Sold

Tie-breaker:
Total Sales Amount

Possible stable final tie:
Product ID/name

Dashboard displays up to five:

Rank
Product name
Units sold
Sales amount

Example:

1. USTP Uniform
25 sold
₱2,500

2. Coca Cola
18 sold
₱450

If only 2 products have sales:
show only 2.

Do NOT invent fake products to make 5.

## TOP-SELLING VALID TRANSACTIONS

Prefer:

Completed
+
Confirmed/Paid
=
valid sale

Do not count:

- Pending
- Processing
- Ready for Pickup but unpaid
- Cancelled
- failed/unconfirmed payment

Use the existing actual project statuses.

Inspect models first.

Do not guess status strings.

## TOP-SELLING VARIANTS

For the main product ranking:

Aggregate variants into their parent product where appropriate.

Example:

USTP Uniform Small = 5
USTP Uniform Medium = 8
USTP Uniform Large = 7

Top product:

USTP Uniform
20 sold

Detailed Analytics may show variant-level performance separately.

## TOP-SELLING BUNDLES

A predefined bundle may count as its own sellable product.

Example:

Super Meal
15 sold

It can appear in Top-Selling Products.

Do not automatically count those 15 bundle sales as 15 independent sales of every component for this product ranking unless the existing business definition explicitly requires it.

Inventory still deducts bundle components.

completed + paid marketplace orders.

## PRODUCT MANAGEMENT

Product statuses:

- Pending
- Approved
- Rejected
- Archived

SUPER ADMIN CREATION:

Super Admin
→ Add Product
→ Save
→ Automatically Approved
→ Visible in Marketplace

No Pending/Approve step for Super Admin-created products.

SELLER CREATION:

Seller
→ Add Product
→ Submit
→ Pending
→ Super Admin review
→ Approve or Reject

If Rejected:
store rejection reason.

Seller may:
edit
resubmit.

5. Description

## COMBO / BUNDLE PRODUCT

A Combo / Bundle Product is one sellable product/listing.

Example:

Super Meal
₱65

Includes:
- Barbecue x1
- Siomai x3
- Banana Cue x1

Bundle form should show:

Included Products
Regular Combined Price
Bundle Price
Customer Savings
Bundle Availability
Description

When Bundle is selected:
generally hide normal standalone Stock Quantity and standard Variants unless the existing system has a justified use.

## BUNDLE SELLER RULE

Bundle components must come from the same seller/product owner.

Do NOT allow:

Seller A product
+
Seller B product

in the same bundle.

For Super Admin:

Seller / Product Owner
must be selected.

Then:
+ Add Products

should show only that seller's approved products.

For seller mobile:
logged-in seller is automatically the owner.

## BUNDLE AVAILABILITY

Bundle stock is derived from component stock.

Do NOT give the bundle fake independent stock.

Formula:

Bundle Availability =
min(
floor(component available stock / required quantity)
)

Example:

Barbecue:
20 available
requires 1
20 / 1 = 20

Siomai:
30 available
requires 3
30 / 3 = 10

Banana Cue:
12 available
requires 1
12 / 1 = 12

Bundle Availability:
10

When 1 bundle is sold:

deduct:
Barbecue x1
Siomai x3
Banana Cue x1

## BUNDLE IMAGE

Preferred:
Seller uploads a real bundle photo showing the actual items together.

Optional fallback:
Create a simple collage using existing product images.

Do NOT require a sketch.

Possible interface:

Bundle Image

○ Upload Bundle Photo
○ Create from Product Images

## PRODUCT IMAGE TOOLS

CampusHub may support:

- Auto Remove Background
- White Studio Background
- Light Gray Studio
- Soft Shadow
- Custom Background
- AI Studio Background

Prefer simple consistent product imagery.

A preset studio-background workflow may be cheaper and easier than generating a completely new AI background every time.

## INVENTORY

Inventory stays as a separate Marketplace sidebar module.

Products and Inventory are related but not the same.

Products:
- listing
- image
- category
- description
- price
- variants
- approval

Inventory:
- physical/available stock state

Recommended Inventory columns:

Product
Variant
On Hand
Reserved
Available
Sold
Status

Definitions:

On Hand:
Physical unsold inventory.

Reserved:
Units allocated to active orders.

Available:
On Hand - Reserved

Sold:
Historical completed sales.

Formula:

Available = On Hand - Reserved

Do NOT add warehouse/storage concepts unless CampusHub really operates a warehouse.

## ORDERS

Order lifecycle:

Pending
→ Processing
→ Ready for Pickup
→ Completed

Alternative:
Cancelled

Payment status is separate.

Pending:
Unpaid

Processing:
Unpaid

Ready for Pickup:
Unpaid

Completed:
Paid

Keep separate:

Order Status
Payment Status

## ORDERS TABLE

Recommended:

#
ORDER ID
CUSTOMER
ITEMS
QTY
TOTAL
ORDER STATUS
PAYMENT STATUS
ORDER DATE
ACTIONS

For multiple items:

Coca Cola + 2 more

Do not force one product name per order.

Buyer checkout should remain simple.

Recommended:

Seller / shop

Product name
Variant
Quantity controls
Add note

Buyer information

Voucher if implemented

Product Subtotal

Cash on Pickup

Total

Total Amount
Place Order

Do not add:
delivery fee
platform fee

unless officially required.

## VOUCHERS

Vouchers are a separate Marketplace function.

Do not embed full voucher creation in Add Product.

Sidebar:
Vouchers

Fields:

Voucher Name
Voucher Code
Description
Discount Type
Discount Value
Minimum Purchase
Maximum Discount
Applies To

End Date

Usage Limit Per User
Status

Applies To:

- All Products
- Selected Products
- Selected Category

Created By:
stored automatically in backend.

Seller-created vouchers:
only apply to that seller's products.

No free-shipping voucher unless the project introduces delivery.

## SELLERS

Seller privilege is separate from general account status.

Potential seller statuses:

Pending
Active
Suspended
Revoked

Prefer suspend/revoke over destructive deletion.

## FACILITIES

CampusHub supports different facility/service workflows.

Examples:

- Covered Court
- Function Hall
- Hostel
- Food Analysis Hub
- Training Kitchen
- Assessment Center
- Commercial/Canteen/Stall Lease

Facility statuses:

- Open for Booking
- Temporarily Unavailable
- Under Maintenance
- Inactive

IMPORTANT:

Facility status and time-slot availability are different.

A facility can be Open for Booking while some slots are occupied.

## GENERAL BOOKING FLOW

Request Submitted
→ Under Review
→ Approved
→ Payment Recorded
→ Reserved / Confirmed
→ Completed

Alternative:
Rejected

Backend must validate booking conflicts.

If a requested schedule is unavailable:
CampusHub may recommend an alternative schedule.

## FACILITY WORKFLOW TYPES

Covered Court / Function Hall:
date/time/slot/event reservation.

Hostel:
room accommodation.

Food Analysis Hub:
service workflow.

Training Kitchen / Assessment Center:
assessment/schedule workflow.

Commercial/Canteen/Stall:
lease application.

Do not force all facility types into one identical booking form if their workflows differ.

## FOOD ANALYSIS WORKFLOW

High-level process:

User submits request
→ Cashier checks
→ BFPT Admin verifies date/facilitator/lab availability
→ unavailable = return reason
→ available = approve schedule
→ user is informed
→ user sends physical sample
→ analyses/services selected
→ cashier confirms sample/service
→ laboratory performs analysis
→ BFPT Admin records/results
→ cashier informs user
→ amount/payment recorded
→ Certificate of Analysis released
→ Completed

Do not simplify this to a standard room-booking flow.

## FACILITY ANALYTICS

Dashboard Facility Analytics may include:

- Booking Overview
- Facility Utilization
- Most Booked Facility
- Upcoming Reservations
- Facility Status
- Facility Revenue

Facility Utilization:

Approved Reserved Hours
/
Available Operating Hours
× 100

Example:

Covered Court

12 booked hours / 48 available hours
25% utilization

Facility Revenue:
count recorded/confirmed payments only.

An approved booking is not automatically revenue.

## APRlORI / ASSOCIATION RULE MINING

CampusHub uses Apriori for:

Frequently Bought Together

This is different from Top-Selling Products.

Libraries:

pandas
mlxtend
openpyxl

Pipeline:

Completed Transactions
→ group items by order
→ clean invalid/cancelled data
→ basket format
→ one-hot encoding
→ Apriori
→ frequent itemsets
→ association_rules
→ filter rules
→ save accepted rules
→ display Frequently Bought Together

Metrics:

Support
Confidence
Lift

Prefer positive associations:
lift > 1

Also use a minimum absolute co-occurrence threshold so that tiny sample sizes do not produce misleading recommendations.

## TOP SELLING VS APRIORI

TOP-SELLING PRODUCTS:

Individual product ranking.

No product pair required.

One Burger sold alone:
Burger sold += 1

FREQUENTLY BOUGHT TOGETHER:

Needs multiple products appearing together in valid transaction baskets.

Example:

Order 1:
Burger + Coke

Order 2:
Burger + Coke

Order 3:
Burger + Fries

Apriori may find:

Burger → Coke

These must remain separate functions.

## BUNDLES AND APRIORI

Predefined bundle components should NOT be treated as organically chosen together.

Example:

Super Meal =
Barbecue
Siomai
Banana Cue

Buyer purchases:

Super Meal
+
Iced Tea

Apriori basket should be:

["Super Meal", "Iced Tea"]

NOT:

["Barbecue", "Siomai", "Banana Cue", "Iced Tea"]

Otherwise Apriori would falsely learn an association that was forced by the predefined bundle.

## APRlORI TESTING

During development:
sample/Kaggle data can be used to prove the algorithm works.

Production:
use actual CampusHub completed orders.

System testing:
create around 30–50+ completed multi-item test transactions.

Example:

10 orders:
Barbecue + Siomai

8 orders:
Barbecue + Coke

6 orders:
Siomai + Banana Cue

4 orders:
Burger + Coke

others:
mixed combinations

Use Completed valid transactions only.

Exclude:

Pending
Cancelled
Rejected
Abandoned

## APRlORI EXECUTION

Do NOT necessarily run Apriori after every single order.

Recommended:

Testing:
manual generation button

Production:
scheduled or threshold-based recomputation

Examples:

nightly
weekly
or after enough new completed orders

Then:

Completed Orders
→ Apriori
→ generate rules
→ filter by support/confidence/lift
→ save accepted rules
→ display FBT

## DSS / RECOMMENDATION FEATURES

CampusHub may provide:

- Demand Forecasting
- Restock Recommendation
- Frequently Bought Together
- Seasonal/Event Demand Forecasting
- Peak Sales Period
- Alternative Schedule Recommendation

Restock formula:

Reorder Point =
Average Daily Demand × Lead Time + Safety Stock

Possible output:

Current Stock
Average Daily Demand
Days to Stockout
Recommended Restock Date
Suggested Restock Quantity

DSS should SUPPORT decisions.

Do not automatically make management decisions for the user.

Avoid vague UI labels:

AI Insight
AI Recommendation
DSS Card

Prefer:

Demand Forecast
Restock Recommendation
Frequently Bought Together
Peak Sales Period

date filter

Dashboard = overview.
Analytics = deeper analysis.
Reports = export/printable output.

Do not duplicate the exact same page three times.

## DATA INTEGRITY

Never fake:

- KPI values
- sales
- revenue
- orders
- bookings
- inventory
- charts
- recommendations
- Apriori results
- forecast results

Loading state is NOT zero.

API error is NOT zero.

Loading:
show skeleton.

"Unable to load data."
Retry

Valid zero:
show actual 0 / ₱0.00 / zero-value chart.

Suspend / Revoke

Orders:
preserve historical records

Bookings:
preserve historical records

Do not unnecessarily delete transaction history.

## PERFORMANCE

Use Django ORM efficiently.

Possible tools:

aggregate()
annotate()
Count()
Sum()
Avg()
Q()
F()
TruncDate()
TruncWeek()
TruncMonth()
select_related()
prefetch_related()

Avoid:

- N+1 queries
- one query per KPI card if unnecessary
- one query per chart point
- loading entire order tables into Python just to calculate aggregates

Use database aggregation when appropriate.

- reasonable touch targets
- responsive layout

Use environment variables.

## CODEX WORKFLOW RULES

Whenever I ask you to modify CampusHub:

1. Read AGENTS.md if it exists.
2. Read CAMPUSHUB_CONTEXT.md if it exists.
3. Inspect relevant files first.
4. Trace frontend → API → model/database when needed.
5. Do not guess.
6. Reuse existing code.
7. Make the smallest safe change.
8. Preserve current business logic unless the task explicitly changes it.
9. Do not introduce duplicate endpoints/pages unnecessarily.
10. Do not overengineer.
11. Do not replace real data with mocks.
12. Do not redesign unrelated screens.

## CODE CHANGE REPORTING FORMAT

EVERY time you make or propose code changes, tell me:

Folder:
<folder>

File:
<file>

Line:
<approximate line>

Nearby anchor:
<class/function/template/component>

Then explain:

- What changed
- Why it changed
- Expected behavior
- How to test it
- Any limitation/assumption

Line numbers may move, so ALWAYS include a nearby code anchor.

## WHEN YOU NEED CLARIFICATION

Do not immediately ask me for information that can be discovered from the repository.

First inspect:

- models
- serializers
- views
- services
- URLs
- templates
- JavaScript
- CSS
- Flutter screens/controllers
- migrations
- tests

Ask me only when:

- a business policy is genuinely ambiguous
- multiple conflicting implementations exist
- institutional policy is not present in the repository
- an irreversible/destructive action requires confirmation

## FINAL INSTRUCTION

Before working on any task, briefly identify:

- which module is affected
- which files are likely involved
- which backend models/services need verification
- which existing business rules must remain intact

Then inspect the code.

Do not start by replacing entire files unless necessary.

Prefer precise, minimal, maintainable changes.

CampusHub business rules and actual repository implementation take priority over generic assumptions.
